is closed\n74: \tif podInfo == nil || podInfo.Pod == nil {\n75: \t\treturn\n76: \t}\n77: \tif podInfo.NeedsPodGroupScheduling {\n78: \t\tpodGroupInfo, err := sched.podGroupInfoForPod(ctx, podInfo)\n79: \t\tif err != nil {\n80: \t\t\tpodFwk, err := sched.frameworkForPod(podInfo.Pod)\n81: \t\t\tif err != nil {\n82: \t\t\t\t// This shouldn't happen, because we only accept for scheduling the pods\n83: \t\t\t\t// which specify a scheduler name that matches one of the profiles.\n84: \t\t\t\tklog.FromContext(ctx).Error(err, \"Error occurred\")\n85: \t\t\t\tsched.SchedulingQueue.Done(podInfo.Pod.UID)\n86: \t\t\t\treturn\n87: \t\t\t}\n88: \t\t\tsched.FailureHandler(ctx, podFwk, podInfo, fwk.AsStatus(err), nil, time.Now())\n89: \t\t\treturn\n90: \t\t}\n91: \t\tsched.scheduleOnePodGroup(ctx, podGroupInfo)\n92: \t} else {\n93: \t\tsched.scheduleOnePod(ctx, podInfo)\n94: \t}\n95: }\n96: \n97: // scheduleOnePod does the entire scheduling workflow for a single pod.\n98: func (sched *Scheduler) scheduleOnePod(ctx context.Context, podInfo *framework.QueuedPodInfo) {\n99: \tlogger := klog.FromContext(ctx)\n100: \tpod := podInfo.Pod\n101: \t// TODO(knelasevero): Remove duplicated keys from log entry calls\n102: \t// When contextualized logging hits GA\n103: \t// https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/issues/111672\n104: \tlogger = klog.LoggerWithValues(logger, \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n105: \tctx = klog.NewContext(ctx, logger)\n106: \tlogger.V(4).Info(\"About to try and schedule pod\", \"pod\", klog.KObj(pod))\n107: \n108: \tfwk, err := sched.frameworkForPod(pod)\n109: \tif err != nil {\n110: \t\t// This shouldn't happen, because we only accept for scheduling the pods\n111: \t\t// which specify a scheduler name that framework.NewCycleState()\n127: \t// For the sake of performance, scheduler does not measure and export the scheduler_plugin_execution_duration metric\n128: \t// for every plugin execution in each scheduling cycle. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01Eq8ptcWca3DvqZ9uJeS1zL","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01CUrbVuBpu2ydSP2qpwUrQg","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# Kubernetes Scheduler Architecture Analysis\n\n## Files Examined\n\n### Core Scheduler\n- **pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go** — Main scheduler struct, initialization, and Run() entry point that starts the scheduling loop\n- **pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go** — Core scheduling pipeline including ScheduleOne(), schedulingCycle(), bindingCycle(), and schedulePod() methods\n\n### Scheduling Queue & Cache\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/queue/scheduling_queue.go** — SchedulingQueue interface with activeQ, backoffQ, unschedulablePods management\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/cache/cache.go** — In-memory cluster state cache with assume/forget mechanisms for optimistic binding\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/cache/snapshot.go** — Immutable snapshot of cache state used during scheduling\n\n### Plugin Framework\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/interface.go** — Framework interfaces and helper types (NodeToStatus, etc.)\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/framework.go** — FrameworkImpl that orchestrates plugin execution at all extension points\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/cycle_state.go** — CycleState for storing pod-specific state during a scheduling cycle\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/registry.go** — Plugin registry for loading and initializing plugins\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/waiting_pods_map.go** — Tracking pods waiting in Permit plugin\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/pods_in_prebind_map.go** — Tracking pods in PreBind phase for preemption\n\n### Supporting Components\n- **pkg/scheduler/profile/** — Scheduler profiles for multi-scheduler scenarios\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/api_dispatcher/** — Async API call dispatcher\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/workloadmanager/** — Workload-aware scheduling support\n- **pkg/scheduler/metrics/** — Metrics collection for scheduler operations\n\n## Dependency Chain\n\n### 1. Entry Point & Initialization\n```\nScheduler.Run() [pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go:536]\n  ↓\n  Starts SchedulingQueue.Run() to accept pods\n  ↓\n  Spawns goroutine: wait.UntilWithContext(ctx, sched.ScheduleOne, 0)\n```\n\n### 2. Main Scheduling Loop\n```\nScheduler.ScheduleOne() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:66]\n  ↓\n  Calls NextPod() (which is podQueue.Pop())\n  ├─→ Returns next pod from SchedulingQueue\n  └─→ Dispatches to either:\n      ├─ scheduleOnePod() [for individual pods]\n      └─ scheduleOnePodGroup() [for workload-aware groups]\n```\n\n### 3. Scheduling Cycle (Synchronous - Find Node)\n```\nscheduleOnePod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:98]\n  ↓\n  1. Creates CycleState: framework.NewCycleState()\n  2. Calls schedulingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:174]\n     ↓\n     Calls schedulingAlgorithm() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:249]\n     ↓\n     Calls sched.SchedulePod() → schedulePod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:562]\n     ↓\n     a) updateSnapshot() - Snapshot current cache state\n     ↓\n     b) findNodesThatFitPod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:626]\n        ↓\n        • RunPreFilterPlugins() [checks pod-level constraints]\n        • findNodesThatPassFilters() [applies Filter plugins to nodes]\n          ├─ parallelized RunFilterPlugins() on candidate nodes\n          └─ applies Extenders for filtering\n        • findNodesThatPassExtenders() [applies Extender filters]\n        ↓\n        Returns: feasibleNodes (nodes that passed all filters)\n     ↓\n     c) prioritizeNodes() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:604]\n        ↓\n        • RunPreScorePlugins() [normalize scores]\n        • parallelized RunScorePlugins() on feasible nodes\n        • applies Extenders for prioritization\n        ↓\n        Returns: priorityList (nodes with scores)\n     ↓\n     d) Selects best node from priorityList\n     ↓\n     Returns: ScheduleResult with SuggestedHost\n  ↓\n  3. Calls prepareForBindingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:197]\n     ↓\n     a) assumeAndReserve() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:306]\n        ├─ assume() [sets NodeName in cache, updates snapshot]\n        └─ RunReservePluginsReserve() [reserve resources]\n     ↓\n     b) RunPermitPlugins() [blocking admission control]\n        └─ Can approve, wait, or reject scheduling\n```\n\n### 4. Binding Cycle (Asynchronous - Bind to API)\n```\nrunBindingCycle() [async goroutine, pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:150]\n  ↓\n  bindingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:390]\n  ↓\n  1. WaitOnPermit() - Wait for permit plugins if needed\n  ↓\n  2. RunPreBindPlugins() - Final pre-binding checks\n  ↓\n  3. bind() - Create actual Pod.Binding to API server\n  ↓\n  4. RunPostBindPlugins() - Post-binding actions\n  ↓\n  On failure: unreserveAndForget() - Clean up assumed state\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### Architecture Overview\n\nThe Kubernetes scheduler uses a **plugin framework** architecture with two clearly separated phases:\n\n#### Phase 1: Scheduling Cycle (Synchronous)\n- **Objective**: Find a suitable node for the pod\n- **Location**: `schedulingCycle()` in `schedule_one.go`\n- **Characteristics**:\n  - Runs to completion synchronously\n  - Works with cluster snapshot (not live state)\n  - May run preemption (PostFilter plugins)\n  - Uses \"assume\" mechanism: pod added to cache **before binding**\n  - Allows next pod to be scheduled while binding is still in progress\n  - Executes serially: only one pod scheduled at a time\n\n#### Phase 2: Binding Cycle (Asynchronous)\n- **Objective**: Persist pod-to-node binding to API server\n- **Location**: `bindingCycle()` in `schedule_one.go`\n- **Characteristics**:\n  - Runs asynchronously in goroutine\n  - Can fail without blocking other scheduling\n  - Includes Permit plugins that can force waiting\n  - If binding fails, RunUnreservePlugins() cleans up resources\n\n### Plugin Framework (Extension Points)\n\nThe framework orchestrates plugins at 14 extension points across the scheduling and binding cycles:\n\n#### Pre-scheduling Phase\n1. **PreEnqueue** — Filters pods before they enter the queue\n2. **QueueSort** — Determines pod ordering in the queue\n\n#### Scheduling Cycle Phase\n3. **PreFilter** — Pod-level checks before filtering nodes\n   - Can filter out nodes at pod level\n   - Results influence which nodes are considered\n4. **Filter** — Node-level feasibility check (parallelized)\n   - Returns: feasible nodes that satisfy pod requirements\n   - Examples: PodFitsResources, NodeUnschedulable, NoVolumeZoneConflict\n5. **PostFilter** — Triggered when pod is unschedulable\n   - Handles preemption attempts\n   - Can return nominated node for retry\n6. **PreScore** — Prepare for scoring phase\n7. **Score** — Rank feasible nodes (parallelized)\n   - Returns: numeric score for each node\n   - Examples: ImageLocality, InterPodAffinity, TaintToleration\n8. **NormalizeScore** — Normalize scores from different plugins\n9. **Reserve** — Resource reservation (first mutating extension point)\n   - Reserves resources in cache\n   - Must be paired with Unreserve for cleanup\n10. **Permit** — Final admission control, can block/wait\n    - Can approve, reject, or wait for external signal\n    - Last point to reject pod during scheduling\n\n#### Binding Cycle Phase\n11. **PreBind** — Final pre-binding actions\n    - Examples: volume mounting, network setup\n    - Can still reject pod\n12. **Bind** — Create actual Pod.Binding to API\n    - Default: DefaultBinder plugin calls API server\n    - Only one BindPlugin can succeed per cycle\n13. **PostBind** — Post-binding cleanup and logging\n    - Cannot fail or reject\n\n#### Additional\n14. **PreBindPreFlight** — Preflight checks before PreBind phase\n\n### Plugin Execution Model\n\n**CycleState**: Thread-safe state container (uses sync.Map) for passing data between plugins within a single scheduling cycle:\n- Written once in PreFilter/PreScore phases\n- Read many times in Filter/Score phases\n- Prevents repeated computation\n\n**Parallelization**:\n- Filter plugins run in parallel across nodes (limited by parallelism factor)\n- Score plugins run in parallel across nodes\n- Filter uses a configurable subset of nodes (not all nodes checked)\n- Can evaluate as few as `minFeasibleNodesToFind` (100) nodes\n\n**Plugin Ordering**:\n- Within each extension point, plugins execute in configured order\n- First plugin failure stops the phase (in Filter/PreBind)\n- Score plugins always run to completion, scores are normalized\n\n### Key Data Structures\n\n#### SchedulingQueue (Prioritized Pod Queue)\n```\nSchedulingQueue = activeQ + backoffQ + unschedulablePods\n- activeQ: Pods ready for scheduling (priority ordered)\n- backoffQ: Pods waiting for backoff period (unschedulable retry delay)\n- unschedulablePods: Pods that failed scheduling, only move on cluster events\n```\n\nEvents cause pods to move between queues based on QueueingHints.\n\n#### Cache (Scheduler's View of Cluster)\n```\nCache maintains:\n- assumedPods: Set of pods that have been assumed (scheduled but not bound)\n- podStates: Pod scheduling state\n- nodes: Node information with doubly-linked list (MRU tracking)\n- Snapshot: Immutable view of cache for scheduling\n```\n\nThe **assume** mechanism allows:\n1. Pod added to cache immediately after scheduling decision\n2. NextPod() can get next pod even if previous binding hasn't completed\n3. Binding failure triggers ForgetPod() to revert cache state\n4. This enables high scheduling throughput\n\n#### CycleState\n```\nPer-pod state for one scheduling/binding cycle:\n- storage: sync.Map for thread-safe pod-specific data\n- recordPluginMetrics: Sample plugin metrics\n- skipFilterPlugins: Plugins to skip in Filter phase\n- skipScorePlugins: Plugins to skip in Score phase\n- parallelPreBindPlugins: PreBind plugins that can run in parallel\n```\n\n### Complete Flow Example\n\n```\nUser creates Pod\n  ↓\nEvent handler adds Pod to SchedulingQueue (activeQ)\n  ↓\nScheduler.ScheduleOne() pops pod\n  ↓\nSCHEDULING CYCLE (synchronous):\n  1. CreateSnapshot from cache\n  2. RunPreFilterPlugins(pod) → get node candidates\n  3. For each candidate node in parallel:\n     - RunFilterPlugins(pod, node) → feasible nodes\n  4. If no feasible nodes:\n     - RunPostFilterPlugins(pod) → attempt preemption\n       └─ May return nominated node\n  5. For each feasible node in parallel:\n     - RunScorePlugins(pod, node) → scores\n  6. Select highest-scored node\n  7. RunReservePlugins(pod, node) → reserve resources in cache\n  8. assume(pod, node) → update snapshot with NodeName set\n  9. RunPermitPlugins(pod, node) → can wait/reject\n  ↓\n  If scheduling succeeded, launch async goroutine:\n  ↓\nBINDING CYCLE (asynchronous):\n  1. WaitOnPermit(pod) → wait for permit plugins\n  2. RunPreBindPlugins(pod, node) → final checks\n  3. Call Bind plugin:\n     - DefaultBinder creates Pod.Binding in API server\n  4. RunPostBindPlugins(pod, node) → logging/cleanup\n  ↓\nBinding succeeds → Pod scheduled\nBinding fails → RunUnreservePlugins() → ForgetPod() → Pod back to queue\n```\n\n### Design Patterns Identified\n\n1. **Two-Phase Scheduling**: Separate find (sync) and bind (async) phases enable concurrent scheduling\n\n2. **Assume Pattern**: Optimistically assume binding will succeed, use snapshot isolation to avoid conflicts\n\n3. **Plugin Registry**: All scheduling logic pluggable via framework interface (no hardcoded algorithm)\n\n4. **Extension Points**: Strategic points throughout pipeline where plugins can be injected\n\n5. **Snapshot Isolation**: Work with immutable snapshots to avoid race conditions while async binding occurs\n\n6. **Parallelization**: Filter and Score phases parallelized across nodes to handle large clusters\n\n7. **Queueing Hints**: Plugins provide hints about which cluster events should requeue specific pods\n\n8. **Preemption Support**: PostFilter plugins attempt preemption when pod is unschedulable\n\n### Configuration & Profiles\n\n- **Multiple Profiles**: Different schedulers can run with different configurations\n- **Extenders**: Legacy support for out-of-tree filtering/prioritizing\n- **Feature Gates**: Enable/disable features like SchedulerAsyncAPICalls, GenericWorkload, OpportunisticBatching\n\n### Concurrency & Performance\n\n- **Parallelism**: Filter and Score plugins use configurable thread pool\n- **Percentage of Nodes to Score**: Optimization to evaluate only a percentage of feasible nodes\n- **Metrics Sampling**: Only sample plugin metrics on ~10% of cycles to reduce overhead\n- **Async Binding**: Binding happens asynchronously so scheduler doesn't wait for API responses\n- **Async API Calls**: Optional APIDispatcher for batched/async API calls (SchedulerAsyncAPICalls feature)\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Kubernetes scheduler implements a sophisticated plugin framework with a **two-phase design**: a synchronous scheduling cycle that finds a suitable node using a series of filtering and scoring plugins, followed by an asynchronous binding cycle that persists the decision to the API server. The \"assume\" mechanism enables concurrent scheduling by optimistically updating an in-memory cache before binding completes, allowing the next pod to be scheduled while binding is still in progress. The plugin framework with 14 extension points enables full customization of scheduling behavior without modifying core scheduler code, while parallelization of filter and score plugins enables scalability to large clusters.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":10,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1612,"cache_read_input_tokens":79085,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1612},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"92796b67-a797-42ac-b6f8-6244a1ac1300","uuid":"bda5d1fd-5bf3-4ce5-b043-10c77f75fd2d"}

Kubernetes Scheduler Architecture Analysis\n\n## Files Examined\n\n### Core Scheduler\n- **pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go** — Main scheduler struct, initialization, and Run() entry point that starts the scheduling loop\n- **pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go** — Core scheduling pipeline including ScheduleOne(), schedulingCycle(), bindingCycle(), and schedulePod() methods\n\n### Scheduling Queue & Cache\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/queue/scheduling_queue.go** — SchedulingQueue interface with activeQ, backoffQ, unschedulablePods management\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/cache/cache.go** — In-memory cluster state cache with assume/forget mechanisms for optimistic binding\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/cache/snapshot.go** — Immutable snapshot of cache state used during scheduling\n\n### Plugin Framework\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/interface.go** — Framework interfaces and helper types (NodeToStatus, etc.)\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/framework.go** — FrameworkImpl that orchestrates plugin execution at all extension points\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/cycle_state.go** — CycleState for storing pod-specific state during a scheduling cycle\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/registry.go** — Plugin registry for loading and initializing plugins\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/waiting_pods_map.go** — Tracking pods waiting in Permit plugin\n- **pkg/scheduler/framework/runtime/pods_in_prebind_map.go** — Tracking pods in PreBind phase for preemption\n\n### Supporting Components\n- **pkg/scheduler/profile/** — Scheduler profiles for multi-scheduler scenarios\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/api_dispatcher/** — Async API call dispatcher\n- **pkg/scheduler/backend/workloadmanager/** — Workload-aware scheduling support\n- **pkg/scheduler/metrics/** — Metrics collection for scheduler operations\n\n## Dependency Chain\n\n### 1. Entry Point & Initialization\n```\nScheduler.Run() [pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go:536]\n  ↓\n  Starts SchedulingQueue.Run() to accept pods\n  ↓\n  Spawns goroutine: wait.UntilWithContext(ctx, sched.ScheduleOne, 0)\n```\n\n### 2. Main Scheduling Loop\n```\nScheduler.ScheduleOne() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:66]\n  ↓\n  Calls NextPod() (which is podQueue.Pop())\n  ├─→ Returns next pod from SchedulingQueue\n  └─→ Dispatches to either:\n      ├─ scheduleOnePod() [for individual pods]\n      └─ scheduleOnePodGroup() [for workload-aware groups]\n```\n\n### 3. Scheduling Cycle (Synchronous - Find Node)\n```\nscheduleOnePod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:98]\n  ↓\n  1. Creates CycleState: framework.NewCycleState()\n  2. Calls schedulingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:174]\n     ↓\n     Calls schedulingAlgorithm() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:249]\n     ↓\n     Calls sched.SchedulePod() → schedulePod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:562]\n     ↓\n     a) updateSnapshot() - Snapshot current cache state\n     ↓\n     b) findNodesThatFitPod() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:626]\n        ↓\n        • RunPreFilterPlugins() [checks pod-level constraints]\n        • findNodesThatPassFilters() [applies Filter plugins to nodes]\n          ├─ parallelized RunFilterPlugins() on candidate nodes\n          └─ applies Extenders for filtering\n        • findNodesThatPassExtenders() [applies Extender filters]\n        ↓\n        Returns: feasibleNodes (nodes that passed all filters)\n     ↓\n     c) prioritizeNodes() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:604]\n        ↓\n        • RunPreScorePlugins() [normalize scores]\n        • parallelized RunScorePlugins() on feasible nodes\n        • applies Extenders for prioritization\n        ↓\n        Returns: priorityList (nodes with scores)\n     ↓\n     d) Selects best node from priorityList\n     ↓\n     Returns: ScheduleResult with SuggestedHost\n  ↓\n  3. Calls prepareForBindingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:197]\n     ↓\n     a) assumeAndReserve() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:306]\n        ├─ assume() [sets NodeName in cache, updates snapshot]\n        └─ RunReservePluginsReserve() [reserve resources]\n     ↓\n     b) RunPermitPlugins() [blocking admission control]\n        └─ Can approve, wait, or reject scheduling\n```\n\n### 4. Binding Cycle (Asynchronous - Bind to API)\n```\nrunBindingCycle() [async goroutine, pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:150]\n  ↓\n  bindingCycle() [pkg/scheduler/schedule_one.go:390]\n  ↓\n  1. WaitOnPermit() - Wait for permit plugins if needed\n  ↓\n  2. RunPreBindPlugins() - Final pre-binding checks\n  ↓\n  3. bind() - Create actual Pod.Binding to API server\n  ↓\n  4. RunPostBindPlugins() - Post-binding actions\n  ↓\n  On failure: unreserveAndForget() - Clean up assumed state\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### Architecture Overview\n\nThe Kubernetes scheduler uses a **plugin framework** architecture with two clearly separated phases:\n\n#### Phase 1: Scheduling Cycle (Synchronous)\n- **Objective**: Find a suitable node for the pod\n- **Location**: `schedulingCycle()` in `schedule_one.go`\n- **Characteristics**:\n  - Runs to completion synchronously\n  - Works with cluster snapshot (not live state)\n  - May run preemption (PostFilter plugins)\n  - Uses \"assume\" mechanism: pod added to cache **before binding**\n  - Allows next pod to be scheduled while binding is still in progress\n  - Executes serially: only one pod scheduled at a time\n\n#### Phase 2: Binding Cycle (Asynchronous)\n- **Objective**: Persist pod-to-node binding to API server\n- **Location**: `bindingCycle()` in `schedule_one.go`\n- **Characteristics**:\n  - Runs asynchronously in goroutine\n  - Can fail without blocking other scheduling\n  - Includes Permit plugins that can force waiting\n  - If binding fails, RunUnreservePlugins() cleans up resources\n\n### Plugin Framework (Extension Points)\n\nThe framework orchestrates plugins at 14 extension points across the scheduling and binding cycles:\n\n#### Pre-scheduling Phase\n1. **PreEnqueue** — Filters pods before they enter the queue\n2. **QueueSort** — Determines pod ordering in the queue\n\n#### Scheduling Cycle Phase\n3. **PreFilter** — Pod-level checks before filtering nodes\n   - Can filter out nodes at pod level\n   - Results influence which nodes are considered\n4. **Filter** — Node-level feasibility check (parallelized)\n   - Returns: feasible nodes that satisfy pod requirements\n   - Examples: PodFitsResources, NodeUnschedulable, NoVolumeZoneConflict\n5. **PostFilter** — Triggered when pod is unschedulable\n   - Handles preemption attempts\n   - Can return nominated node for retry\n6. **PreScore** — Prepare for scoring phase\n7. **Score** — Rank feasible nodes (parallelized)\n   - Returns: numeric score for each node\n   - Examples: ImageLocality, InterPodAffinity, TaintToleration\n8. **NormalizeScore** — Normalize scores from different plugins\n9. **Reserve** — Resource reservation (first mutating extension point)\n   - Reserves resources in cache\n   - Must be paired with Unreserve for cleanup\n10. **Permit** — Final admission control, can block/wait\n    - Can approve, reject, or wait for external signal\n    - Last point to reject pod during scheduling\n\n#### Binding Cycle Phase\n11. **PreBind** — Final pre-binding actions\n    - Examples: volume mounting, network setup\n    - Can still reject pod\n12. **Bind** — Create actual Pod.Binding to API\n    - Default: DefaultBinder plugin calls API server\n    - Only one BindPlugin can succeed per cycle\n13. **PostBind** — Post-binding cleanup and logging\n    - Cannot fail or reject\n\n#### Additional\n14. **PreBindPreFlight** — Preflight checks before PreBind phase\n\n### Plugin Execution Model\n\n**CycleState**: Thread-safe state container (uses sync.Map) for passing data between plugins within a single scheduling cycle:\n- Written once in PreFilter/PreScore phases\n- Read many times in Filter/Score phases\n- Prevents repeated computation\n\n**Parallelization**:\n- Filter plugins run in parallel across nodes (limited by parallelism factor)\n- Score plugins run in parallel across nodes\n- Filter uses a configurable subset of nodes (not all nodes checked)\n- Can evaluate as few as `minFeasibleNodesToFind` (100) nodes\n\n**Plugin Ordering**:\n- Within each extension point, plugins execute in configured order\n- First plugin failure stops the phase (in Filter/PreBind)\n- Score plugins always run to completion, scores are normalized\n\n### Key Data Structures\n\n#### SchedulingQueue (Prioritized Pod Queue)\n```\nSchedulingQueue = activeQ + backoffQ + unschedulablePods\n- activeQ: Pods ready for scheduling (priority ordered)\n- backoffQ: Pods waiting for backoff period (unschedulable retry delay)\n- unschedulablePods: Pods that failed scheduling, only move on cluster events\n```\n\nEvents cause pods to move between queues based on QueueingHints.\n\n#### Cache (Scheduler's View of Cluster)\n```\nCache maintains:\n- assumedPods: Set of pods that have been assumed (scheduled but not bound)\n- podStates: Pod scheduling state\n- nodes: Node information with doubly-linked list (MRU tracking)\n- Snapshot: Immutable view of cache for scheduling\n```\n\nThe **assume** mechanism allows:\n1. Pod added to cache immediately after scheduling decision\n2. NextPod() can get next pod even if previous binding hasn't completed\n3. Binding failure triggers ForgetPod() to revert cache state\n4. This enables high scheduling throughput\n\n#### CycleState\n```\nPer-pod state for one scheduling/binding cycle:\n- storage: sync.Map for thread-safe pod-specific data\n- recordPluginMetrics: Sample plugin metrics\n- skipFilterPlugins: Plugins to skip in Filter phase\n- skipScorePlugins: Plugins to skip in Score phase\n- parallelPreBindPlugins: PreBind plugins that can run in parallel\n```\n\n### Complete Flow Example\n\n```\nUser creates Pod\n  ↓\nEvent handler adds Pod to SchedulingQueue (activeQ)\n  ↓\nScheduler.ScheduleOne() pops pod\n  ↓\nSCHEDULING CYCLE (synchronous):\n  1. CreateSnapshot from cache\n  2. RunPreFilterPlugins(pod) → get node candidates\n  3. For each candidate node in parallel:\n     - RunFilterPlugins(pod, node) → feasible nodes\n  4. If no feasible nodes:\n     - RunPostFilterPlugins(pod) → attempt preemption\n       └─ May return nominated node\n  5. For each feasible node in parallel:\n     - RunScorePlugins(pod, node) → scores\n  6. Select highest-scored node\n  7. RunReservePlugins(pod, node) → reserve resources in cache\n  8. assume(pod, node) → update snapshot with NodeName set\n  9. RunPermitPlugins(pod, node) → can wait/reject\n  ↓\n  If scheduling succeeded, launch async goroutine:\n  ↓\nBINDING CYCLE (asynchronous):\n  1. WaitOnPermit(pod) → wait for permit plugins\n  2. RunPreBindPlugins(pod, node) → final checks\n  3. Call Bind plugin:\n     - DefaultBinder creates Pod.Binding in API server\n  4. RunPostBindPlugins(pod, node) → logging/cleanup\n  ↓\nBinding succeeds → Pod scheduled\nBinding fails → RunUnreservePlugins() → ForgetPod() → Pod back to queue\n```\n\n### Design Patterns Identified\n\n1. **Two-Phase Scheduling**: Separate find (sync) and bind (async) phases enable concurrent scheduling\n\n2. **Assume Pattern**: Optimistically assume binding will succeed, use snapshot isolation to avoid conflicts\n\n3. **Plugin Registry**: All scheduling logic pluggable via framework interface (no hardcoded algorithm)\n\n4. **Extension Points**: Strategic points throughout pipeline where plugins can be injected\n\n5. **Snapshot Isolation**: Work with immutable snapshots to avoid race conditions while async binding occurs\n\n6. **Parallelization**: Filter and Score phases parallelized across nodes to handle large clusters\n\n7. **Queueing Hints**: Plugins provide hints about which cluster events should requeue specific pods\n\n8. **Preemption Support**: PostFilter plugins attempt preemption when pod is unschedulable\n\n### Configuration & Profiles\n\n- **Multiple Profiles**: Different schedulers can run with different configurations\n- **Extenders**: Legacy support for out-of-tree filtering/prioritizing\n- **Feature Gates**: Enable/disable features like SchedulerAsyncAPICalls, GenericWorkload, OpportunisticBatching\n\n### Concurrency & Performance\n\n- **Parallelism**: Filter and Score plugins use configurable thread pool\n- **Percentage of Nodes to Score**: Optimization to evaluate only a percentage of feasible nodes\n- **Metrics Sampling**: Only sample plugin metrics on ~10% of cycles to reduce overhead\n- **Async Binding**: Binding happens asynchronously so scheduler doesn't wait for API responses\n- **Async API Calls**: Optional The \"assume\" mechanism enables concurrent scheduling by optimistically updating an in-memory cache before binding completes, allowing the next pod to be scheduled while binding is still in progress.
